## Review

**7 out of 10, Effective.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

This is a competent investor update built to be read as a document. The route is clear: the agenda on slide 2 maps to the sections, highlights come first, then the summary tables, then supporting detail and the statements. The main weakness is that most pages carry a generic label (Financial Summary, Core Technology) rather than a message title, so the reader has to find the takeaway in dense prose, as on slides 3, 6 and 10. The tables are tidy and the Q4 2023 column is highlighted, but the tables on slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 have no callouts, and the charts on slides 10 to 11 and 22 to 23 have little emphasis beyond a red bar or a single color change.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the agenda on slide 2, the pink highlight on the current-period column in the tables, the red final bar on the cost per vehicle chart on slide 10, and the full-bleed photos with short captions on slides 14 to 20.

**Most useful repair:** Replace the topic labels with one-sentence takeaway titles, cut the prose on slides 3, 6 and 10 to the few points that matter, and raise the footnote and small table text on slides 6, 9 and 25 to 30 to a readable size.

**What would change the score:** I would add direct takeaways to the key metrics charts on slides 22 and 23, fix the crowded rotated axis labels on slide 10, and drop or compress the photo run of slides 14 to 21 that does not link to the numbers.

_Confidence high. 32 of 32 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a Highlights slide that lists profitability, cash and operations results for Q4 and full-year 2023 in short bullet points. It then moves through a Financial Summary and an Operational Summary, with quarterly and annual tables for revenue, margins, cash flow, deliveries, production and deployments. Next comes Vehicle Capacity, a table of installed capacity by factory, followed by Core Technology and Other Highlights, which cover FSD Beta, cost of goods sold per vehicle, energy storage and services. An Outlook slide sets expectations for 2024 volume, cash and product plans. A photo and chart section shows vehicles, factories and product lines. The closing sections hold key metrics charts, full financial statements with income statement, balance sheet and cash flow, reconciliations of GAAP to non-GAAP measures, and a legal page with webcast details, definitions and forward-looking statement risks. Slides 3, 6, 10 and 11 are worth studying for how each slide pairs a single headline message with a supporting chart or table.

## Questions about this deck

**How many pages does the Tesla Q4 and FY 2023 update deck have?**

The deck has 32 pages, and the contents slide on slide 2 lists its sections from Highlights on slide 3 through Additional Information on slide 31.

**What does the Tesla Q4 and FY 2023 deck say about 2023 operating income and net income?**

Slide 3 reports $8.9B in GAAP operating income in 2023 and $15.0B in GAAP net income in 2023. Slide 5 shows the same full-year figures in its financial summary table.

**What does the Tesla Q4 and FY 2023 deck say about full-year 2023 deliveries?**

Slide 8 lists total deliveries of 1,808,581 in 2023, up 38% from 2022. Slide 8 also shows Model 3/Y deliveries of 1,739,707 for the year.

**What does the Tesla Q4 and FY 2023 deck say about energy storage deployments in 2023?**

Slide 3 states an energy storage deployment of 14.7 GWh in 2023, a 125% growth. Slide 8 lists storage deployed as 14,724 MWh for 2023, with the same 125% YoY change.

- text at x 50.0, y 65.4, w 43.3, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: fleet of over a million vehicles, this system uses AI to influence vehicle controls
- text at x 50.0, y 68.0, w 42.3, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: (steering wheel, pedals, indicators, etc.) instead of hard-coding every driving
- text at x 50.0, y 70.6, w 32.3, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: behavior. V12 marks a new era in the path to full autonomy.
- text at x 4.4, y 75.3, w 6.1, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: Operations
- text at x 4.4, y 75.3, w 6.1, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: Operations

- text at x 37.8, y 51.0, w 0.4, h 1.2 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.20; #FFFFFF]: 2
- text at x 50.0, y 75.8, w 44.4, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: We are focused on bringing the next generation platform to market as quickly as
- text at x 50.0, y 78.4, w 44.2, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: we can, with the plan to start production at Gigafactory Texas. This platform will
- text at x 66.6, y 52.4, w 0.4, h 1.2 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.20; #FFFFFF]: 5
- text at x 13.3, y 80.7, w 34.0, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: Energy storage deployment of 14.7 GWh in 2023, 125% growth
- text at x 50.0, y 81.0, w 24.7, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #FFFFFF]: revolutionize how vehicles are manufactured.

**Layout**

Single full-width data table on a white page. The section label FINANCIAL SUMMARY with the sub-label (Unaudited) sits top-left at about 9% from the top, aligned to the table's left edge at 4.6% margin. The table spans from 4.6% to 95.4% horizontally and from 13.9% to about 89.6% vertically, with 21 rows plus a header row, a blank gap row between each group, and six numeric columns with the label column on the left. Page number 4 sits bottom-left at about 95% from the top; a faint Tesla wordmark sits bottom-right. No slide title in large type and no footer text beyond the page number and wordmark.

**Visuals**

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean white financial-table style with no decoration. Header row fill grey #7F7F7F with white text. Body rows alternate between light grey #F2F2F2 and white, with 1 px white gutters between cells and rows. The Q4-2023 column is tinted pale rose #F2D7D7 across its body cells. Section label FINANCIAL SUMMARY in Gotham SSm Medium, widely letter-spaced capitals, dark grey #333333, about 1.7% of slide height (cap height roughly 1.2%). Sub-label (Unaudited) in Gotham SSm Medium at the same size, #333333. Table text in Gotham SSm Book, about 1.8% of slide height, black #000000 for body numbers and labels, white for header cells. Numbers right-aligned, labels left-aligned. Page number in Gotham SSm Medium about 1.6%, light grey #BABABA. No rounded corners, shadows or gradients.

**Layout**

Single full-width table on a white slide with a small left-aligned title block at the top-left and a table starting at about 4.6% from the left edge. The title FINANCIAL SUMMARY sits at about 9.3% from the top with the subtitle (Unaudited) beneath it. The table fills the width from about 4.6% to 95.4% and runs down to about 89.5% of the height. The page number 5 sits bottom-left at about 3% from the left and 95% from the top, and a faint Tesla wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white slide with no ornament. Title FINANCIAL SUMMARY in dark grey #333333, widely letter-spaced, Gotham SSm Medium style geometric sans-serif, all capitals, about 1.7% of slide height; subtitle (Unaudited) in the same colour and family, about 1.7%. Table body text is black #000000 in Gotham SSm Book, a geometric sans-serif, regular weight, about 1.8% of slide height. Header text is white on mid-grey #7F7F7F. Row bands #F2F2F2 alternate with white. The 2023 column is highlighted in pale pink. Lines are absent, cells are flat rectangles with square corners, no shadows or gradients. Page number in light grey #BABABA, Gotham SSm Medium, about 1.6%. Tesla logo very light grey.

### Slide 6

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Pairs each financial section label with short bullets that explain YoY drivers using plus and minus signs, a clear way to explain a number.
- Review: The main weakness is that most pages carry a generic label (Financial Summary, Core Technology) rather than a message title, so the reader has to find the takeaway in dense prose, as on slides 3, 6 and 10.
- Suggested repair: Replace the topic labels with one-sentence takeaway titles, cut the prose on slides 3, 6 and 10 to the few points that matter, and raise the footnote and small table text on slides 6, 9 and 25 to 30 to a readable size.

**Text**

FINANCIAL SUMMARY / Revenue / Total revenue grew 3% YoY in Q4 to $25.2B. YoY, revenue was impacted by the following items: / + growth in vehicle deliveries / + growth in other parts of the business / + positive FX impact of $0.1B1 / - reduced vehicle average selling price (ASP) YoY (excl. FX impact), including unfavorable impact of mix / - lower FSD revenue recognition YoY due to FSD Beta wide release in North America in Q4`22 / Profitability / Our operating income decreased YoY to $2.1B in Q4, resulting in an 8.2% operating margin. YoY, operating income was / primarily impacted by the following items: / - reduced vehicle ASP due to pricing and mix / - increase in operating expenses partly driven by AI and other R&D projects / - lower FSD revenue recognition YoY due to FSD Beta wide release in North America in Q4`22 / - cost of Cybertruck production ramp / + lower cost per vehicle, including lower raw material costs, logistics costs and IRA credit benefit / + growth in vehicle deliveries / + gross profit growth in Energy Generation and Storage / While it did not impact Operating Income, we did record a one-time non-cash tax benefit of $5.9B in Q4 for the release of / valuation allowance on certain deferred tax assets. / Cash / Quarter-end cash, cash equivalents and investments increased sequentially by $3.0B to $29.1B in Q4, driven by free cash / flow of $2.1B and financing activities of $0.9B. / 6 / (1) Impact is calculated on a constant currency basis. Actuals are compared against current results converted into USD using average exchange rates from Q4’22. / TESLA wordmark (no text other than the logo)

**Layout**

Two-column text layout on a white page: a letter-spaced section title sits top-left at about 9.3% from the top with a left margin of about 4.5%. Row labels (Revenue, Profitability, Cash) form a narrow left column starting at about 4.5% of the width, and the body text runs in a wide right column starting at about 19.3% of the width, top-aligned with each label. A footnote sits at the bottom left with the page number 6 at the far left margin, and a faint Tesla wordmark sits at the bottom right.

**Visuals**

None apart from the faint Tesla wordmark logo at bottom right, drawn as widely letter-spaced light grey capitals T E S L A in a thin sans-serif, about 5.5% of the width. No charts, photographs, tables or diagrams.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradient, shadow or ornament. Section title is GothamSSm-Medium, letter-spaced capitals, colour #333333. Row labels are GothamSSm-Book in #111111. Introductory paragraphs and the closing paragraphs are GothamSSm-Book in grey #6E6E6E. Bullet lines are GothamSSm-Book in black #000000 with no bullet glyphs, the leading + or - characters acting as markers. Body text and labels are about 1.79% of slide height; the title is about 1.74% of slide height with capitals. Footnote and footer are about 1.35% and 1.58% of slide height in light greys #BFBFBF and #BABABA. Lines are set at roughly 2.6% of slide height apart; there are no rules, boxes or accent colours.

- text at x 19.3, y 18.7, w 51.8, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #6E6E6E]: Total revenue grew 3% YoY in Q4 to $25.2B. YoY, revenue was impacted by the following items:

- text at x 19.3, y 26.5, w 20.9, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #000000]: + growth in other parts of the business
- text at x 19.3, y 29.0, w 15.8, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #000000]: + positive FX impact of $0.1B
- text at x 19.3, y 31.6, w 56.4, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #000000]: - reduced vehicle average selling price (ASP) YoY (excl. FX impact), including unfavorable impact of mix
- text at x 19.3, y 34.2, w 50.8, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #000000]: - lower FSD revenue recognition YoY due to FSD Beta wide release in North America in Q4`22
- text at x 4.5, y 42.8, w 6.6, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #111111]: Profitability
- text at x 4.5, y 42.8, w 6.6, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #111111]: Profitability
- text at x 19.3, y 43.0, w 49.1, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #6E6E6E]: Our operating income decreased YoY to $2.1B in Q4, resulting in an 8.2% operating margin.
- text at x 35.0, y 29.0, w 0.3, h 1.2 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.20; #000000]: 1
- text at x 68.7, y 43.0, w 15.1, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #6E6E6E]: YoY, operating income was

- text at x 19.3, y 50.8, w 24.4, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #000000]: - reduced vehicle ASP due to pricing and mix
- text at x 19.3, y 53.4, w 40.7, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #000000]: - increase in operating expenses partly driven by AI and other R&D projects
- text at x 19.3, y 56.0, w 50.8, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #000000]: - lower FSD revenue recognition YoY due to FSD Beta wide release in North America in Q4`22

- text at x 19.3, y 61.1, w 52.6, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #000000]: + lower cost per vehicle, including lower raw material costs, logistics costs and IRA credit benefit
- text at x 19.3, y 63.7, w 16.1, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #000000]: + growth in vehicle deliveries
- text at x 19.3, y 66.3, w 30.1, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #000000]: + gross profit growth in Energy Generation and Storage
- text at x 19.3, y 71.5, w 66.0, h 1.8 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.79; #6E6E6E]: While it did not impact Operating Income, we did record a one-time non-cash tax benefit of $5.9B in Q4 for the release of

**Layout**

A single left-aligned title block sits at the top left (OPERATIONAL SUMMARY with a subtitle (Unaudited) beneath it, starting about 9 percent from the top and 4.6 percent from the left). Below it a full-width data table spans from about x 4.6 to 95.4 percent, with a grey header row and a stack of banded rows grouped into sections separated by empty gaps; numeric columns are right-aligned. A footnote runs along the bottom left above the slide number 7 at the bottom left corner, and a faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom right, both at about 94 percent from the top.

**Visuals**

A single data table with no chart, photo or icon. Columns: row label column (no header text, about 4.6 to 43.3 percent wide), then Q4-2022, Q1-2023, Q2-2023, Q3-2023, Q4-2023 and YoY. Rows in order: Model 3/Y production (419,088; 421,371; 460,211; 416,800; 476,777; 14%); Other models production (20,613; 19,437; 19,489; 13,688; 18,212; -12%); Total production in bold (439,701; 440,808; 479,700; 430,488; 494,989; 13%); empty spacer row; Model 3/Y deliveries (388,131; 412,180; 446,915; 419,074; 461,538; 19%); Other models deliveries (17,147; 10,695; 19,225; 15,985; 22,969; 34%); Total deliveries in bold (405,278; 422,875; 466,140; 435,059; 484,507; 20%); indented row of which subject to operating lease accounting (15,184; 22,357; 21,883; 17,423; 10,563; -30%); empty spacer; Total end of quarter operating lease vehicle count (140,667; 153,988; 168,058; 176,231; 176,564; 26%); Global vehicle inventory (days of supply) with superscript (1) (13; 15; 16; 16; 15; 15%); empty spacer; Solar deployed (MW) (100; 67; 66; 49; 41; -59%); Storage deployed (MWh) (2,462; 3,889; 3,653; 3,980; 3,202; 30%); empty spacer; Tesla locations (963; 1,000; 1,068; 1,129; 1,208; 25%); Mobile service fleet (1,584; 1,692; 1,769; 1,846; 1,909; 21%); empty spacer; Supercharger stations (4,678; 4,947; 5,265; 5,595; 5,952; 27%); Supercharger connectors (42,419; 45,169; 48,082; 51,105; 54,892; 29%). The Q4-2023 column cells are shaded light pink as a highlight column. The table is not cropped and is boxed only by its row fills.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradient or texture. Title is set in widely letter-spaced uppercase grey sans-serif, about 1.7 percent of slide height, with the subtitle in regular weight of the same size. Table text is a geometric sans-serif (Gotham-style) in black #000000 at about 1.8 percent of slide height, with bold for total rows. Header row text is white #FFFFFF in book weight. Accents are light pink #F2DCDB-style highlight cells in the Q4-2023 column. Row fills are light grey #F2F2F2 alternating with white gaps; header fill is mid grey #7F7F7F. No borders, corner radii, shadows or gradients on the table. Footnote is light grey #BFBFBF at about 1.35 percent of slide height. Page number is #BABABA in medium weight. Wordmark is very pale grey.

**Layout**

A single full-width table occupies the upper-middle of a white slide, starting at the left margin of about 4.6% of slide width with the section heading 'OPERATIONAL SUMMARY' and '(Unaudited)' stacked above it at top-left. Column headers run across a grey bar; the first column holds row labels left-aligned and the remaining six columns hold right-aligned figures. Footnotes sit bottom-left with the page number '8' to their left at the margin, and a small faint Tesla wordmark sits bottom-right; there is no title beyond the spaced caps heading.

**Visuals**

Table with 7 columns: an unlabelled row-label column, then 2019, 2020, 2021, 2022, 2023 and YoY. Rows in order: Model 3/Y production 302,301 / 454,932 / 906,032 / 1,298,434 / 1,775,159 / 37%. Other models production 62,931 / 54,805 / 24,390 / 71,177 / 70,826 / 0%. Total production (bold) 365,232 / 509,737 / 930,422 / 1,369,611 / 1,845,985 / 35%. Blank spacer row. Total deliveries (bold) 367,656 / 499,647 / 936,222 / 1,313,851 / 1,808,581 / 38%. Indented sub-row 'of which subject to operating lease accounting' 25,439 / 34,470 / 60,912 / 47,582 / 72,226 / 52%. Blank spacer row. Total end of year operating lease vehicle count 49,901 / 72,089 / 120,342 / 140,667 / 176,564 / 26%. Global vehicle inventory (days of supply)(1) 13 / 15 / 6 / 16 / 16 / 0%. Blank spacer row. Solar deployed (MW) 173 / 205 / 345 / 348 / 223 / -36%. Storage deployed (MWh) 1,651 / 3,022 / 3,992 / 6,541 / 14,724 / 125%. Blank spacer row. Tesla locations(2) 433 / 523 / 644 / 963 / 1,208 / 25%. Mobile service fleet 758 / 894 / 1,281 / 1,584 / 1,909 / 21%. Blank spacer row. Supercharger stations 1,821 / 2,564 / 3,476 / 4,678 / 5,952 / 27%. Supercharger connectors 16,104 / 23,277 / 31,498 / 42,419 / 54,892 / 29%. No charts, photographs or illustrations. The 2023 column cells are highlighted in pale pink, and the 2023 values for Total production and Total deliveries are bold; no other cells are highlighted.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white slide with no decoration. Headline 'OPERATIONAL SUMMARY' is letter-spaced uppercase in a geometric sans (Gotham Small Medium-style) in dark grey #333333, about 1.74% of slide height, with '(Unaudited)' below in the same family at the same size. Table text is Gotham Small Book in black #000000 at about 1.79% of slide height, with bold rows in Gotham Small Bold. Header text is white Gotham Small Book on mid-grey #7F7F7F. Alternate row bands are very light grey #F2F2F2 with white gaps between row groups. The 2023 column highlight is a pale pink around #F2D7D7. Rectangular cells with no rounded corners, no shadows, no gradients and no rules beyond the cell fills. Footnotes are Gotham Small Book in light grey #BFBFBF at about 1.35% of slide height, page number in Gotham Small Medium #BABABA at about 1.58%.

### Slide 9

**Review notes**

- Suggested repair: Replace the topic labels with one-sentence takeaway titles, cut the prose on slides 3, 6 and 10 to the few points that matter, and raise the footnote and small table text on slides 6, 9 and 25 to 30 to a readable size.

**Text**

VEHICLE CAPACITY / After our scheduled global factory shutdown in Q3, our global production reached a record annualized run rate of nearly 2.0 million vehicles in Q4. The refreshed Model 3 - with a significantly quieter cabin, ventilated seats, a screen for rear seats, longer range and many other improvements - is now available globally. / US: California, Nevada and Texas / Before Tesla purchased the Fremont factory, the record output of the previous owner was nearly 430,000 vehicles made in a single year. In 2023, the Tesla Fremont factory produced nearly 560,000 vehicles thanks to our ~20,000 Fremont-based employees. At Gigafactory Texas, we began production of the Cybertruck and delivered the first units to customers. We expect the ramp of Cybertruck to be longer than other models given its manufacturing complexity. / China: Shanghai / Shanghai resumed normal rate production in Q4, rebounding from the scheduled downtime in Q3. Production of the updated Model 3 ramped to full speed in less than two months. / Europe: Berlin-Brandenburg / Model Y production in Berlin continued to grow in Q4, achieving both a record weekly production rate and a sequential increase in total production volume for the seventh consecutive quarter. / Current Installed Annual Vehicle Capacity / Region | Model | Capacity | Status / California | Model S / Model X | 100,000 | Production / (blank) | Model 3 / Model Y | 550,000 | Production / Shanghai | Model 3 / Model Y | >950,000 | Production / Berlin | Model Y | 375,000 | Production / Texas | Model Y | >250,000 | Production / (blank) | Cybertruck | >125,000 | Production / Nevada | Tesla Semi | - | Pilot production / Various | Next Gen Platform | - | In development / TBD | Roadster | - | In development / Installed capacity ≠ current production rate and there may be limitations discovered as production rates approach capacity. Production rates depend on a variety of factors, including equipment uptime, component supply, downtime related to factory upgrades, regulatory considerations and other factors. / Market share of Tesla vehicles by region (TTM) / US/Canada / Europe / China / 4% / 3% / 2% / 1% / 0% / Q4 2017 / Q1 2018 / Q2 2018 / Q3 2018 / Q4 2018 / Q1 2019 / Q2 2019 / Q3 2019 / Q4 2019 / Q1 2020 / Q2 2020 / Q3 2020 / Q4 2020 / Q1 2021 / Q2 2021 / Q3 2021 / Q4 2021 / Q1 2022 / Q2 2022 / Q3 2022 / Q4 2022 / Q1 2023 / Q2 2023 / Q3 2023 / Q4 2023 / Source: Tesla estimates based on latest available data from ACEA; Autonews.com; CAAM – light-duty vehicles only; TTM = Trailing twelve months / 9 / T E S L A

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a white page: a left text column from about 4.6% to 50% of slide width holding four stacked sections separated by hairline rules, and a right column from about 54.6% to 95.5% holding the capacity table at top and the market-share line chart below it. The letter-spaced title VEHICLE CAPACITY sits top-left at about 9.3% of slide height, left-aligned to the 4.6% margin. The page number 9 sits bottom-left at about 95% height, and the source line sits bottom-right beneath the chart, with a faint wordmark at the bottom-right corner.

**Visuals**

Two visuals. (1) Table titled Current Installed Annual Vehicle Capacity with four columns (Region, Model, Capacity, Status) and nine body rows: California | Model S / Model X | 100,000 | Production; blank region | Model 3 / Model Y | 550,000 | Production; Shanghai | Model 3 / Model Y | >950,000 | Production; Berlin | Model Y | 375,000 | Production; Texas | Model Y | >250,000 | Production; blank region | Cybertruck | >125,000 | Production; Nevada | Tesla Semi | - | Pilot production; Various | Next Gen Platform | - | In development; TBD | Roadster | - | In development. Header row is grey with white labels; body rows are light grey with thin white separators; Capacity values are right-aligned, Status values centred. (2) Line chart titled Market share of Tesla vehicles by region (TTM). X axis: quarters Q4 2017 to Q4 2023 (25 quarterly points, labels rotated about 45 degrees). Y axis: 0% to 4% in 1% steps, light grey horizontal gridlines. Legend above plot: US/Canada (blue line), Europe (red line), China (green line). No values are printed on the chart; approximate readings: US/Canada rises from about 0.25% in Q4 2017 to about 1.0% in Q4 2018, about 1.1% in Q2 2020, about 2.3% in Q4 2021, about 3.6% in Q4 2022 and about 3.95% in Q4 2023. Europe is about 0.2% through 2018, about 0.8% in Q1 2020, about 1.2% in Q4 2021, about 2.0% in Q4 2022 and about 2.85% in Q4 2023. China is about 0.05% in 2017, about 0.9% in Q1 2021, about 1.6% in Q4 2021, about 1.85% in Q4 2022 and about 2.35% in Q4 2023. No highlighted points. No photographs or logos except the faint wordmark.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradients or textures. Title in dark grey #333333, Gotham-style geometric sans-serif, medium weight, all caps with very wide letter-spacing, about 1.7% of slide height. Footnote and source text in pale grey #BFBFBF, about 1.3% of slide height. Dividers in light grey #D9D9D9, hairline. No corner radii, shadows or gradients; all shapes are square-cornered.

### Slide 10

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Places a single trend chart beside a short narrative, so the chart carries the argument on the cost-per-vehicle point.
- Review: The main weakness is that most pages carry a generic label (Financial Summary, Core Technology) rather than a message title, so the reader has to find the takeaway in dense prose, as on slides 3, 6 and 10.
- Review: The tables are tidy and the Q4 2023 column is highlighted, but the tables on slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 have no callouts, and the charts on slides 10 to 11 and 22 to 23 have little emphasis beyond a red bar or a single color change.
- Worth keeping: Keep the agenda on slide 2, the pink highlight on the current-period column in the tables, the red final bar on the cost per vehicle chart on slide 10, and the full-bleed photos with short captions on slides 14 to 20.
- Suggested repair: Replace the topic labels with one-sentence takeaway titles, cut the prose on slides 3, 6 and 10 to the few points that matter, and raise the footnote and small table text on slides 6, 9 and 25 to 30 to a readable size.
- What would change the score: I would add direct takeaways to the key metrics charts on slides 22 and 23, fix the crowded rotated axis labels on slide 10, and drop or compress the photo run of slides 14 to 21 that does not link to the numbers.

**Text**

CORE TECHNOLOGY
Artificial Intelligence Software and Hardware
In Q4, we released our latest FSD Beta software (V12) to select Tesla employees, and more recently, to customers. V12 utilizes end-to-end training, enhancing the driving experience. We also introduced the 2nd generation of the Optimus robot, which uses Tesla-designed actuators and sensors and improved AI capabilities. Both FSD Beta and Optimus are trained with similar technology pillars: real-world data, neural net training and cutting-edge hardware and software.
Vehicle and Other Software
We delivered the Cybertruck with an all-new user interface and new communication bus, Etherloop, that efficiently moves data while significantly reducing wiring. As part of our holiday update, when parking, Tesla vehicles without ultrasonic sensors display a high-fidelity 3D rendering of the vehicle's surroundings, including nearby barriers, vehicles and painted markings. Wireless Bluetooth headphones can also now be paired with the rear screen when watching shows or playing games. FleetAPI now enables third-party solutions that enhance integration and management of Tesla devices.
Battery, Powertrain & Manufacturing
Cost of goods sold per vehicle declined sequentially to slightly above $36,000. Even as we approach the natural limit of cost down of our existing vehicle lineup, our team continues to focus on further cost reductions across all points of production, from raw materials to final delivery.
Cumulative miles driven with FSD Beta (millions)
Cost of goods sold per vehicle
10
T E S L A

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a white field: a left text column about 4.6% to 50% of the slide width holding three stacked sections separated by two thin grey horizontal rules, and a right column about 54% to 96% holding a line chart on top and a bar chart below. The section label CORE TECHNOLOGY sits top-left at about 9% from the top with a 4.6% left margin. Page number 10 sits bottom-left at about 95% from the top; a faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Two charts. (1) Line chart, titled 'Cumulative miles driven with FSD Beta (millions)' placed below the line, with a y-axis labelled 0 to 800 in steps of 100 (no axis unit title, unit is millions as in chart title) and a categorical x-axis of monthly labels rotated about 45 degrees from Mar-21 through Dec-23 (every month, 34 labels). One series, a thick red line about #C0504D, rising from about 0 at Mar-21 to about 10 by Sep-21, about 30 by Apr-22, about 130 by Mar-23 and about 760 at Dec-23; values are estimates (about). No legend, no highlighted points, no value labels. Gridlines are light grey horizontal lines at each 100 step. (2) Vertical bar chart, titled 'Cost of goods sold per vehicle' below the bars, with y-axis labels $34,000 to $40,000 in steps of $1,000 (axis range $34,000 to $40,000) and x-axis categories Q4 2022, Q1 2023, Q2 2023, Q3 2023, Q4 2023. Bars are dark navy #1F497D for Q4 2022 (about $39,550), Q1 2023 (about $38,500), Q2 2023 (about $37,900) and Q3 2023 (about $37,500); the Q4 2023 bar is red #C0504D at about $36,300 and is the only highlighted point. Values are estimates (about) as the slide prints none. No legend. Light grey horizontal gridlines at each $1,000 step; no axis lines beyond the baseline.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean white corporate slide. Section label CORE TECHNOLOGY in widely letter-spaced uppercase Gotham-style geometric sans-serif, medium weight, dark charcoal #333333, about 1.7% of slide height. Subheadings in Gotham Book sans-serif, near-black #111111, about 1.8% of slide height (cap height about 1.3%). Body text in Gotham Book, mid grey #6E6E6E, about 1.8% of slide height with generous line spacing of about 2.9% of slide height. Chart titles in Gotham Medium, grey #7E7E7E and #7F7F7F, about 1.8%. Axis labels Gotham Book, #595959, about 1.35%. Accents: navy #1F497D bars, red #C0504D line and highlighted bar. Thin light grey #BFBFBF hairline rules, 1 px, separating sections. No shapes with rounded corners, no shadows, no gradients.

### Slide 11

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Uses two side-by-side bar charts of energy storage and services gross profit to show growth without extra text.
- Review: The tables are tidy and the Q4 2023 column is highlighted, but the tables on slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 have no callouts, and the charts on slides 10 to 11 and 22 to 23 have little emphasis beyond a red bar or a single color change.

**Text**

OTHER HIGHLIGHTS / Energy Storage / Energy storage deployments decreased sequentially in Q4 to 3.2 GWh, for a total / deployment of 14.7 GWh in 2023, a 125% increase compared to 2022. While we / expect deployments will continue to be volatile on a sequential basis, impacted by / logistics and the global distribution of projects at any given time, we expect / continued growth on a trailing twelve-month basis going forward. We continue to / ramp our 40 GWh Megafactory in Lathrop, CA toward full capacity. / Solar / Solar deployments declined on a sequential and YoY basis to 41 MW. Downward / pressure on solar demand continued into Q4 as interest rates have remained / high. Profitability in the quarter was negatively impacted by lower deployments and / seasonal weakness in solar energy generation. / Services and Other business / The Services and Other business continued to grow alongside our fleet in 2023, / achieving record revenue and gross profit generation. The biggest drivers of profit / generation in 2023 were part sales, used vehicle sales, merchandise sales and pay- / per-use supercharging. As our fleet continues to expand in the coming years, there / is an opportunity for fleet-related services to become a more meaningful driver of / profit generation. / Energy Storage deployments (GWh) / 16 / 14 / 12 / 10 / 8 / 6 / 4 / 2 / 0 / 2015 / 2016 / 2017 / 2018 / 2019 / 2020 / 2021 / 2022 / 2023 / Services & Other gross profit ($M) / 600 / 500 / 400 / 300 / 200 / 100 / 0 / -100 / -200 / -300 / -400 / -500 / -600 / 2015 / 2016 / 2017 / 2018 / 2019 / 2020 / 2021 / 2022 / 2023 / 11 / T E S L A

The slide title OTHER HIGHLIGHTS sits top-left at y 9.3%, left-aligned to the same margin as the text column (x 4.5%). Thin horizontal rules separate the three text sections. Page number 11 sits bottom-left at x 3%, y 95%; a faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom-right near x 91.5%, y 93%. Margins are about 4.5% left and 4% right, with no header band or footer bar.

**Visuals**

Two vertical bar charts, both on a white background with light grey horizontal gridlines and no plot border. Chart 1 (top right): column chart titled Energy Storage deployments (GWh) placed below the plot, y-axis 0 to 16 GWh in steps of 2, x-axis years 2015 to 2023. Values: 2015 about 0 (barely visible), 2016 about 0.2, 2017 about 0.4, 2018 about 1.0, 2019 about 1.7, 2020 about 3.0, 2021 about 4.0, 2022 about 6.5, 2023 14.7 (14.7 GWh as stated in the body text). Bars are navy #1F497D except the 2023 bar, highlighted in brick red #C0504D. No legend and no data labels. Both charts' axis labels are grey #595959, Gotham Book, about 1.35% of slide height.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean corporate white design. Background pure white #FFFFFF. Title OTHER HIGHLIGHTS in dark charcoal #333333, Gotham Medium, all capitals, widely letter-spaced, about 1.74% of slide height (small caps-style header). ### Slide 12

**Text**

OUTLOOK
Volume
Our company is currently between two major growth waves: the first one began with the global expansion of the / Model 3/Y platform and the next one we believe will be initiated by the global expansion of the next-generation vehicle / platform. In 2024, our vehicle volume growth rate may be notably lower than the growth rate achieved in 2023, as our / teams work on the launch of the next-generation vehicle at Gigafactory Texas. In 2024, the growth rate of deployments / and revenue in our Energy Storage business should outpace the Automotive business.
Cash

Photograph of an unpainted stainless-steel Cybertruck body in a factory. The body is suspended by a red steel jig with two motorised hoist units and red cross-beams from the ceiling. Right: a grey wire-mesh safety cage with a red and white DANGER sign reading AUTHORIZED PERSONNEL ONLY. Background: workers in dark clothing, monitors, racks and overhead lights. Lighting is neutral overhead industrial light, with soft reflections on the steel. Dominant colours are steel grey, signal red, concrete grey and white. The photo is rectangular, not cropped, not full-bleed, with no overlay or tint. A yellow and black striped floor line runs along the bottom edge of the photo.

The enclosure has a row of about nine vertical double doors with black handles and a louvred vent strip along its top. Below it, a row of white cabinets holds stacked orange battery modules in grey frames, with one cabinet open in the centre showing electronics. Cabinets with orange modules continue to the left and right, and a red floor edge runs along the bottom. Background: exposed steel roof trusses, red crane rails, fluorescent lights, a pipe run, and a worker in high-visibility clothing at far left. Composition: eye-level, straight-on, centred, with the suspended enclosure filling the upper half and the cabinets filling the lower half. The photo is not cropped at its edges and is boxed, not full-bleed, with no overlay or tint. Lighting: bright, even factory lighting. Dominant colours: white and light grey, with orange battery modules, red crane and floor accents, and dark steel grey.

### Slide 23

**Review notes**

- Review: The tables are tidy and the Q4 2023 column is highlighted, but the tables on slides 4, 5, 7 and 8 have no callouts, and the charts on slides 10 to 11 and 22 to 23 have little emphasis beyond a red bar or a single color change.
- What would change the score: I would add direct takeaways to the key metrics charts on slides 22 and 23, fix the crowded rotated axis labels on slide 10, and drop or compress the photo run of slides 14 to 21 that does not link to the numbers.

**Text**

KEY METRICS / TRAILING 12 MONTHS (TTM) / (Unaudited) / 2.0 / 1.8 / 1.6 / 1.4 / 1.2 / 1.0 / 0.8 / 0.6 / 0.4 / 0.2 / 0.0 / 1Q-2021 / 2Q-2021 / 3Q-2021 / 4Q-2021 / 1Q-2022 / 2Q-2022 / 3Q-2022 / 4Q-2022 / 1Q-2023 / 2Q-2023 / 3Q-2023 / 4Q-2023 / Vehicle Deliveries / (millions of units) / 20 / 18 / 16 / 14 / 12 / 10 / 8 / 6 / 4 / 2 / 0 / 1Q-2021 / 2Q-2021 / 3Q-2021 / 4Q-2021 / 1Q-2022 / 2Q-2022 / 3Q-2022 / 4Q-2022 / 1Q-2023 / 2Q-2023 / 3Q-2023 / 4Q-2023 / Operating Cash Flow ($B) / Free Cash Flow ($B) / 20 / 18 / 16 / 14 / 12 / 10 / 8 / 6 / 4 / 2 / 0 / 1Q-2021 / 2Q-2021 / 3Q-2021 / 4Q-2021 / 1Q-2022 / 2Q-2022 / 3Q-2022 / 4Q-2022 / 1Q-2023 / 2Q-2023 / 3Q-2023 / 4Q-2023 / Net Income ($B) / Adjusted EBITDA ($B) / 23

**Layout**

Three equal-width vertical bar charts sit side by side in three columns across the middle of the slide, each with its own y-axis at the left, quarter labels rotated beneath, and a caption or legend below. The title block sits top-left at about 4.6% from the left edge and 9.3% from the top, with '(Unaudited)' on a second line beneath it. Page number '23' sits bottom-left at about 3% from the left and 95% from the top; a faint TESLA wordmark sits bottom-right. Gridlines align all three plot areas on the same top and bottom edges.

**Visuals**

Three clustered column charts, all with quarterly categories 1Q-2021 to 4Q-2023 on the x-axis (rotated 90 degrees, grey). Chart 1 (left): single-series column chart titled by caption 'Vehicle Deliveries (millions of units)'. Y-axis 0.0 to 2.0 in steps of 0.2, unit millions of units. Single series in blue, values about 0.59, 0.70, 0.80, 0.93, 1.06, 1.11, 1.21, 1.31, 1.42, 1.63, 1.72, 1.81 (no values are printed; all are estimates). No legend. Chart 2 (middle): two series, Operating Cash Flow ($B) in blue and Free Cash Flow ($B) in red. Y-axis 0 to 20 in steps of 2, unit $B. Operating Cash Flow about 8.0, 9.1, 9.9, 11.4, 13.8, 14.0, 16.0, 14.7, 13.2, 13.9, 12.1, 13.2. Free Cash Flow about 3.9, 4.1, 4.0, 5.0, 6.9, 6.9, 8.9, 7.5, 5.7, 6.1, 3.6, 4.3. Legend below the chart: blue square 'Operating Cash Flow ($B)' above red square 'Free Cash Flow ($B)'. No highlighted points. Chart 3 (right): two series, Net Income ($B) in blue and Adjusted EBITDA ($B) in red. Y-axis 0 to 20 in steps of 2, unit $B. Net Income about 1.1, 2.1, 3.4, 5.5, 8.3, 9.5, 11.1, 12.5, 11.7, 12.1, 10.7, 14.9. Adjusted EBITDA about 6.6, 7.9, 9.3, 11.5, 14.7, 16.0, 17.8, 19.1, 18.3, 19.2, 18.0, 16.5. Legend below: blue square 'Net Income ($B)' above red square 'Adjusted EBITDA ($B)'. No highlighted points. Bars are flat-filled rectangles, about 0.6% of slide width each, with two bars per quarter in the middle and right charts and one per quarter in the left chart; bars are drawn with a subtle vertical gradient, darker at the top of the blue bars. Gridlines run horizontally at each axis tick, light grey, hairline.

**Layout**

A single full-width table block sits in the upper half of a white slide, with the two-line title block stacked above its header row at the top left. The title and table share a left margin of about 4.6% of slide width, the table runs to about 95.5% on the right, and the table occupies rows from about 14.5% to 69.5% of slide height. Nothing sits below the table except the faint page number bottom left and the faint TESLA wordmark bottom right.

**Visuals**

A financial reconciliation table, not a chart. Columns: row label column (left-aligned, about 4.6% to 50% of width), then Q4-2022, Q1-2023, Q2-2023, Q3-2023 and Q4-2023 (right-aligned numeric columns). Header row: 'In millions of USD or shares as applicable, except per share data' followed by the five quarter labels. Section 1 (net income): Net income attributable to common stockholders (GAAP) 3,687 / 2,513 / 2,703 / 1,853 / 7,928; Stock-based compensation expense 419 / 418 / 445 / 465 / 484; Release of valuation allowance on deferred tax assets — / — / — / — / (5,927); Net income attributable to common stockholders (non-GAAP) 4,106 / 2,931 / 3,148 / 2,318 / 2,485; Less: Buy-out of noncontrolling interest (35) / (5) / — / 2 / 1; Net income used in computing diluted EPS attributable to common stockholders (non-GAAP) 4,141 / 2,936 / 3,148 / 2,316 / 2,484. Section 2 (EPS): EPS attributable to common stockholders, diluted (GAAP) 1.07 / 0.73 / 0.78 / 0.53 / 2.27; Stock-based compensation expense per share 0.12 / 0.12 / 0.13 / 0.13 / 0.14; Release of valuation allowance on deferred tax assets per share — / — / — / — / (1.70); EPS attributable to common stockholders, diluted (non-GAAP) 1.19 / 0.85 / 0.91 / 0.66 / 0.71; Shares used in EPS calculation, diluted (GAAP and non-GAAP) 3,471 / 3,468 / 3,478 / 3,493 / 3,492. Section 3 (Adjusted EBITDA): Net income attributable to common stockholders (GAAP) 3,687 / 2,513 / 2,703 / 1,853 / 7,928; Interest expense 33 / 29 / 28 / 38 / 61; Provision for (benefit from) income taxes 276 / 261 / 323 / 167 / (5,752); Depreciation, amortization and impairment 989 / 1,046 / 1,154 / 1,235 / 1,232; Stock-based compensation expense 419 / 418 / 445 / 465 / 484; Adjusted EBITDA (non-GAAP) 5,404 / 4,267 / 4,653 / 3,758 / 3,953; Total revenues 24,318 / 23,329 / 24,927 / 23,350 / 25,167; Adjusted EBITDA margin (non-GAAP) 22.2% / 18.3% / 18.7% / 16.1% / 15.7%. Grey spacer bands separate the three sections. Bold rows are subtotal and total lines. The Q4-2023 column is highlighted in a pale pink tint behind all its cells. No chart, no logos apart from the faint wordmark.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean white corporate financial slide with no decoration. Title is spaced uppercase sans-serif in dark grey #333333, body and table text is black #000000 in a light geometric sans-serif (Gotham Book), bold rows in Gotham Bold. The title is about 1.7% of slide height and table text about 1.5%. Thin light grey horizontal rules separate table rows. Grey spacer bands #F2F2F2 separate the sections. The Q4-2023 column carries a pale pink tint #F5DADA as its accent. Page number and wordmark are very light grey. No shadows, no rounded corners, no gradients.

**Layout**

Single-column financial table on a plain white slide. The letter-spaced all-caps title sits top-left at about 9.4% from the top, with the '(Unaudited)' line beneath it, both left-aligned to a left margin of about 4.6%. The table starts at about 14.5% from the top and spans from the left margin to about 95.4% of the width; the label column is left-aligned and the five year columns (2019 to 2023) are right-aligned. Three light grey spacer bands divide the table into groups, and the 2023 column is shaded pink. The page number sits bottom-left at about 95% from the top and the faint Tesla wordmark sits bottom-right; there is no other footer.

**Visuals**

No photographs, charts or icons. The only non-text visual is a financial table with 6 columns (row label, 2019, 2020, 2021, 2022, 2023) and 22 rows in three groups separated by grey bands. Group 1 (rows 1 to 7) is the net income reconciliation: header row 'In millions of USD or shares as applicable, except per share data'; 'Net (loss) income attributable to common stockholders (GAAP)' (862) / 721 / 5,519 / 12,556 / 14,997; 'Stock-based compensation expense' 898 / 1,734 / 2,121 / 1,560 / 1,812; 'Release of valuation allowance on deferred tax assets' — / — / — / — / (5,927); 'Net income attributable to common stockholders (non-GAAP)' 36 / 2,455 / 7,640 / 14,116 / 10,882; 'Less: Buy-out of noncontrolling interest' 8 / 31 / (5) / (27) / (2); 'Less: Dilutive convertible debt' — / — / (9) / (1) / —; 'Net income used in computing diluted EPS attributable to common stockholders (non-GAAP)' 28 / 2,424 / 7,654 / 14,143 / 10,884. Group 3 (rows 14 to 22) is the Adjusted EBITDA reconciliation: 'Net (loss) income attributable to common stockholders (GAAP)' (862) / 721 / 5,519 / 12,556 / 14,997; 'Interest expense' 685 / 748 / 371 / 191 / 156; 'Provision for (benefit from) income taxes' 110 / 292 / 699 / 1,132 / (5,001); 'Depreciation, amortization and impairment' 2,154 / 2,322 / 2,911 / 3,747 / 4,667; 'Stock-based compensation expense' 898 / 1,734 / 2,121 / 1,560 / 1,812; 'Adjusted EBITDA (non-GAAP)' 2,985 / 5,817 / 11,621 / 19,186 / 16,631; 'Total revenues' 24,578 / 31,536 / 53,823 / 81,462 / 96,773; 'Adjusted EBITDA margin (non-GAAP)' 12.1% / 18.4% / 21.6% / 23.6% / 17.2%. **Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Minimal white corporate financial slide. Background white #FFFFFF. Title and header text in dark grey #333333, medium weight, letter-spaced capitals; table body text is near-black #000000 in Gotham-style book weight, subtotal rows in bold. The only accent is a pale pink #F5DADB fill on the 2023 column, and grey spacer bands #F2F2F2. Typeface is a geometric sans-serif in the Gotham family (GothamSSm Medium for the title, Book for body, Bold for totals). **Layout**

Single white page with the title block at top-left (left margin about 4.6% of width, title top at about 9.4% of height) in letter-spaced capitals with '(Unaudited)' beneath it. Four stacked tables run full width below: left-aligned row labels, right-aligned numeric columns; the first two tables carry 15 quarter columns (2Q-2020 to 4Q-2023) and the last two carry 12 (1Q-2021 to 4Q-2023). Light grey separator bands sit between the blocks. Footer at bottom-left holds the page number 30 and the footnote; a small Tesla wordmark sits at bottom-right.

none

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradient, no imagery and no shadows. Table text in Gotham SSm Book, about 1.46% of slide height, black #000000 for body and figures. Separator bands #F2F2F2 with no outline. Row rules are thin hairlines in light grey. Footer page number and footnote in light grey #999999; the wordmark is pale grey #CCCCCC. Numeric values use parentheses for negatives.

### Slide 31

**Text**

ADDITIONAL INFORMATION
WEBCAST INFORMATION
Tesla will provide a live webcast of its fourth quarter 2023 financial results conference call beginning at 4:30 p.m. CT on January 24, 2024 at ir.tesla.com. This webcast will also be available for replay for approximately one year thereafter.
CERTAIN TERMS
When used in this update, certain terms have the following meanings. Our vehicle deliveries include only vehicles that have been transferred to end customers with all paperwork correctly completed. Our energy product deployment volume includes both customer units installed and equipment sales; we report installations at time of commissioning for storage projects or inspection for solar projects, and equipment sales at time of delivery. "Adjusted EBITDA" is equal to (i) net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders before (ii)(a) interest expense, (b) provision for income taxes, (c) depreciation, amortization and impairment and (d) stock-based compensation expense. "Free cash flow" is operating cash flow less capital expenditures. Average cost per vehicle is cost of automotive sales divided by new vehicle deliveries (excluding operating leases). “Days sales outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts receivable, net for the period divided by (ii) total revenues and multiplied by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days payable outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts payable for the period divided by (ii) total cost of revenues and multiplied by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days of supply” is calculated by dividing new car ending inventory by the relevant quarter’s deliveries and using 75 trading days. Constant currency impacts are calculated by comparing actuals against current results converted into USD using average exchange rates from the prior period.
NON-GAAP FINANCIAL INFORMATION
Consolidated financial information has been presented in accordance with GAAP as well as on a non-GAAP basis to supplement our consolidated financial results. Our non-GAAP financial measures include non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders, non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders on a diluted per share basis (calculated using weighted average shares for GAAP diluted net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders), Adjusted EBITDA, Adjusted EBITDA margin and free cash flow. These non-GAAP financial measures also facilitate management’s internal comparisons to Tesla’s historical performance as well as comparisons to the operating results of other companies. Management believes that it is useful to supplement its GAAP financial statements with this non-GAAP information because management uses such information internally for its operating, budgeting and financial planning purposes. Management also believes that presentation of the non-GAAP financial measures provides useful information to our investors regarding our financial condition and results of operations, so that investors can see through the eyes of Tesla management regarding important financial metrics that Tesla uses to run the business and allowing investors to better understand Tesla’s performance. Non-GAAP information is not prepared under a comprehensive set of accounting rules and therefore, should only be read in conjunction with financial information reported under U.S. GAAP when understanding Tesla’s operating performance. A reconciliation between GAAP and non-GAAP financial information is provided above.
FORWARD-LOOKING STATEMENTS
Certain statements in this update, including statements in the “Outlook” section; statements relating to the future development, ramp, production and capacity, demand and market growth, cost, pricing and profitability, investment, deliveries, deployment, availability and other features and improvements and timing of existing and future Tesla products; statements regarding operating margin, operating profits, spending and liquidity; and statements regarding expansion, improvements and/or ramp and related timing at our factories are “forward-looking statements” that are subject to risks and uncertainties. These forward-looking statements are based on management’s current expectations, and as a result of certain risks and uncertainties, actual results may differ materially from those projected. The following important factors, without limitation, could cause actual results to differ materially from those in the forward-looking statements: the risk of delays in launching and manufacturing our products and features cost-effectively; our ability to grow our sales, delivery, installation, servicing and charging capabilities and effectively manage this growth; consumers’ demand for electric vehicles generally and our vehicles specifically; the ability of suppliers to deliver components according to schedules, prices, quality and volumes acceptable to us, and our ability to manage such components effectively; any issues with lithium-ion cells or other components manufactured at our factories; our ability to ramp our factories in accordance with our plans; our ability to procure supply of battery cells, including through our own manufacturing; risks relating to international expansion; any failures by Tesla products to perform as expected or if product recalls occur; the risk of product liability claims; competition in the automotive and energy product markets; our ability to maintain public credibility and confidence in our long-term business prospects; our ability to manage risks relating to our various product financing programs; the status of government and economic incentives for electric vehicles and energy products; our ability to attract, hire and retain key employees and qualified personnel; our ability to maintain the security of our information and production and product systems; our compliance with various regulations and laws applicable to our operations and products, which may evolve from time to time; risks relating to our indebtedness and financing strategies; and adverse foreign exchange movements. More information on potential factors that could affect our financial results is included from time to time in our Securities and Exchange Commission filings and reports, including the risks identified under the section captioned “Risk Factors” in our annual report on Form 10-K filed with the SEC on January 31, 2023. Tesla disclaims any obligation to update information contained in these forward-looking statements whether as a result of new information, future events or otherwise.
31

Body text in GothamSSm-Book (geometric sans-serif, regular weight) at about 1.35% of slide height, black #000000, justified, with generous line spacing of roughly 1.45 and paragraph gaps of about 1% of slide height. "Adjusted EBITDA" is equal to (i) net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders before (ii)(a) interest expense, (b) provision for income taxes, (c) depreciation, amortization and impairment and (d) stock-based compensation expense. "Free cash flow" is operating cash flow less capital expenditures. Average cost per vehicle is cost of automotive sales divided by new vehicle deliveries (excluding operating leases). “Days sales outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts receivable, net for the period divided by (ii) total revenues and multiplied by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days payable outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts payable for the period divided by (ii) total cost of revenues and multiplied by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days of supply” is calculated by dividing new car ending inventory
- text at x 4.6, y 32.8, w 91.1, h 3.2 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.35; #000000]: by the relevant quarter’s deliveries and using 75 trading days. Constant currency impacts are calculated by comparing actuals against current results converted into USD using average exchange rates from the prior period.

- text at x 4.6, y 40.2, w 90.9, h 14.3 [GothamSSm-Book; font size 1.35; #000000]: Consolidated financial information has been presented in accordance with GAAP as well as on a non-GAAP basis to supplement our consolidated financial results. Our non-GAAP financial measures include non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders, non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders on a diluted per share basis (calculated using weighted average shares for GAAP diluted net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders), Adjusted EBITDA, Adjusted EBITDA margin and free cash flow. These non-GAAP financial measures also facilitate management’s internal comparisons to Tesla’s historical performance as well as comparisons to the operating results of other companies. Management believes that it is useful to supplement its GAAP financial statements with this non-GAAP information because management uses such information internally for its operating, budgeting and financial planning purposes. Management also believes that presentation of the non-GAAP financial measures provides useful information to our investors regarding our financial condition and results of operations, so that investors can see through the eyes of Tesla management regarding important financial metrics that Tesla uses to run the business and allowing investors to better understand Tesla’s performance. Non-GAAP information is not prepared under a comprehensive set of accounting rules and therefore, should only be read in conjunction with financial information reported under U.S. GAAP when understanding Tesla’s operating performance. A reconciliation between GAAP and non-GAAP financial information is provided above.

These forward-looking statements are based on management’s current expectations, and as a result of certain risks and uncertainties, actual results may differ materially from those projected. The following important factors, without limitation, could cause actual results to differ materially from those in the forward-looking statements: the risk of delays in launching and manufacturing our products and features cost-effectively; our ability to grow our sales, delivery, installation, servicing and charging capabilities and effectively manage this growth; consumers’ demand for electric vehicles generally and our vehicles specifically; the ability of suppliers to deliver components according to schedules, prices, quality and volumes acceptable to us, and our ability to manage such components effectively; any issues with lithium-ion cells or other components manufactured at our factories; our ability to ramp our factories in accordance with our plans; our ability to procure supply of battery cells, including through our own manufacturing; risks relating to international expansion; any failures by Tesla products to perform as expected or if product recalls occur; the risk of product liability claims; competition in the automotive and energy product markets; our ability to maintain public credibility and confidence in our long-term business prospects; our ability to manage risks relating to our various product financing programs; the status of government and economic incentives for electric vehicles and energy products; our ability to attract, hire and retain key employees and qualified personnel; our ability to maintain the security of our information and production and product systems; our compliance with various regulations and laws applicable to our operations and products, which may evolve from time to time; risks relating to our indebtedness and financing strategies; and adverse foreign exchange movements. More information on potential factors that could affect our financial results is included from time to time in our Securities and Exchange Commission filings and reports, including the risks identified under the section captioned “Risk Factors” in our annual report on Form 10-K filed with the SEC on January 31, 2023. Tesla disclaims any obligation to update information contained in these forward-looking statements whether as a result of new information, future events or otherwise.

### Slide 32

**Text**

TESLA

**Layout**

A single centred wordmark sits on an otherwise empty dark slide; no title, body text, footer or slide number. The wordmark is horizontally centred and vertically near the middle of the slide with generous margins on all sides.

**Visuals**

One logo: the Tesla wordmark rendered as five widely letter-spaced glyphs in a squared, stylised sans-serif, reading left to right T, E, S, L, A. The glyphs are a grey tone, flat with no gradient or shadow, and are small relative to the slide.

**Design**

Background: Solid near-black #111111 fill covering the entire slide. Near-black background #111111 with the wordmark in a muted mid-grey (about #8A8A8A). No titles or body text. No accent colours, no lines, no shapes, no shadows, no corner radii. Typeface of the wordmark is a custom geometric slab-like logotype rather than a standard font; letters are widely tracked.
